### 1796.

<summary>This title page introduces a polemical documentary account by Leopold Alois Hoffmann, aiming to expose the alleged conspiratorial links between the "German Union" and various secret societies like the Illuminati and Freemasons. Published in Vienna in 1796, it reflects the era's deep anxieties regarding secret organizations and their perceived role in the social and political upheavals following the French Revolution.</summary>

<keywords>German Union, Illuminati, Freemasons, Rosicrucian Order, Leopold Alois Hoffmann, Vienna, 18th-century secret societies, counter-revolutionary literature</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page is the beginning of a preface, designated by the signature A 2. It introduces a supplement to the author's previous memoirs, focusing specifically on a controversial group known as the "German Union."</meta>

A 2

Following the publication of the first part of my highly significant memoirs, a request has been made known to me from many quarters. It has been suggested that for the sake of many readers who lack detailed information regarding the **German Union**, yet who hear it discussed on various occasions, I should publicly share what I know of it, or what I find it appropriate to disclose. What should stop me from fulfilling this request? The most instructive original papers are in my hands; and I understand quite well that in these times—when the <term>Enlightenment</term> <note>The author uses "Aufklärung," referring to the 18th-century intellectual movement, but implies here that its more radical elements are being discredited.</note> of <term>Jacobinism</term> <note>A term for radical revolutionary politics, named after the Jacobin Club of the French Revolution.</note> is finally beginning to be reduced to its true value—these must be made known as the most important historical <term>official records</term> <note>Original German: Aktenstücke; these are primary source documents used as evidence.</note> in the world; for from these the world will—

<summary>In this preface, the author explains his decision to publish original documents concerning the "German Union," a secret society, in order to expose the reality of radical Enlightenment influence during a period of political upheaval.</summary>
<keywords>German Union, Enlightenment, Jacobinism, memoirs, primary sources, secret societies, political history</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's justification for publishing secret documents related to the German Union. It follows the previous page's unfinished thought: "for from these the world will—"</meta>

—be able to see clearly and brightly how it has come to pass that, for the last eight to ten years, a <term>false Enlightenment</term> <note>The author uses "falsche Aufklärung" to describe what he sees as a perversion of the intellectual movement, suggesting it has been used as a tool for subversion rather than true progress.</note> and the despotism of this movement were able to seize a tyrannical supremacy over German literature and public opinion. It will show how it happened that princes, authorities, statesmen, bishops, and indeed all well-meaning and right-thinking men in Germany were kept in the deepest state of unconsciousness regarding the secret course of affairs. They were held in an <term>indolence</term> <note>Original: "Indolenz." In this context, the author refers to a state of pitiable apathy or passive indifference toward a looming political threat.</note> as pitiable as it is incomprehensible, even as moral and political revolutions were being carried out—so to speak—underground and in the very bowels of the earth.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;I wish for every reader to reflect upon these matters with close attention; and in order to provide the most useful pointers at the appropriate places, I shall append various remarks and narrate many facts that have come to my knowledge through a careful combination of circumstances.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The authenticity of the [documents] to be shared—

<summary>The author argues that a radical "false Enlightenment" has covertly dominated German intellectual life for a decade while leaders remained dangerously unaware. He promises to provide commentary and factual evidence to expose these "underground" revolutionary activities.</summary>
<keywords>false Enlightenment, despotism, public opinion, secret societies, political revolution, indolence, German literature, authenticity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical or organizational correspondence typical of late 18th-century Enlightenment circles, likely related to a secret society or intellectual union.</meta>

->Pest <note>Modern-day Budapest, Hungary</note>, June 28th, 1788.<-

A friendly hand has been so kind as to send me a printed sheet addressed to the friends of Reason, Truth, and Virtue. I cannot describe this sheet as anything other than the outlines <note>original: "Lineamente"</note> of a grand design. Since everyone who receives notice of this sheet is permitted to express their views on it, I am writing down my opinion as openly and impartially as is ever allowed for an honest man under such circumstances.

It is irrefutably true that forces eternally struggle against forces in both physical and spiritual nature. Whoever knows the human heart and history knows that the striving for superiority—or, what amounts to the same thing, the feeling of one's own strength—has been the great mainspring <note>original: "Springfeder"</note> of all human action and impact from the very beginning. The weak person is always the poorest on earth; and one is always weak when standing alone on one's own small stage, and

<summary>The author acknowledges receipt of a manifesto regarding a "grand design" for the friends of Reason and Virtue, reflecting on the inherent human struggle for power and the vulnerability of the individual.</summary>
<keywords>Enlightenment, Reason, Truth, Virtue, human nature, power dynamics, Pest, 1788</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>Continuing the author's philosophical reflection on the individual's struggle against societal pressures and the necessity of balancing moral purity with practical wisdom.</meta>

the masses hardly suspect how those further off on their own stage are making plans to bring this individual under their yoke. The consciousness of one's own virtue <term>Tugend: moral excellence and integrity</term> is surely always a beautiful consolation, even for the most solitary man. But even with all this beautiful consolation, the noblest virtue never escapes the superior power of malice <note>original: "Arglust," implying a deceitful or mischievous intent</note>.

The innocence of a dove <note>A biblical allusion to Matthew 10:16</note> gives off too humble a physiognomy, especially in this age of the most industrious cunning. One must arm oneself with some measure of courage. One must believe that great feelings do not reside within a human being simply so that he may stifle them as a sacrifice to the strong. Strength must act against strength, and power against power. The wisdom of a serpent <note>original: "Schlangenklugheit"</note> must guide the steps of the man who lives in a world where openness of heart is gradually becoming nothing more than a good-natured traitor and a deceived spy.

Whoever observes the contemporary history of Germany sees clearly that the features of this portrait are very frequently present there in the original. Things have come so far,

<summary>The author argues that moral virtue alone is insufficient protection against a cunning world, urging the reader to combine "dove-like" innocence with "serpent-like" wisdom to survive social and political malice. He notes that these observations are particularly visible in the current state of German affairs.</summary>
<keywords>virtue, malice, innocence of doves, wisdom of serpents, German history, individual vs. society, moral strength</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's reflection on the social and political instability caused by competing factions, likely following a discussion on the loss of public trust.</meta>

...that one can no longer take a single secure step without bumping into the outposts of some <term>faction<note>The author uses "faction" to describe divisive political or religious groups that prioritize their own interests over the common good.</note></term>. <margin>That was certainly clear!</margin>

Therefore, every honest man should surely work against such factions. His own security and love for his equally honest fellow citizens must compel him to do so. From this perspective, the call to form an association of philanthropic friends of truth is indeed very honorable and highly appropriate for the needs of our time.

However, I find one question unavoidable:
What means will be used to work against such factions in the most certain manner? —

I must ask whether truth and <term>Enlightenment<note>original: "Aufklärung." This refers to the 18th-century intellectual movement emphasizing reason and individual education as tools for social progress.</note></term> can be considered thoroughly effective means for this purpose?

I do not believe that these questions appear premature or impertinent, even if I must confess that they smell a little of <term>skepticism<note>A philosophical approach of doubting certain knowledge; here, the author questions if "reason" alone can fix deep social divides.</note></term>. It is a very sublime...

“Infinitely, worthy man, we rejoice
“to have gained in you such a sympathetic
“member. <margin>Wucherer <note>Probably Johann Ludwig Wucherer, a contemporary clergyman</note> had announced me as such to the Twenty-Two. But I was nothing more than that, until my letter. —the enlightened Sans-culottes <note>A term borrowed from the French Revolution, here used sarcastically to describe radical or 'enlightened' members of the Union</note> who had entered the Union, had to be fed during the prevailing hunger. The dear public must allow itself to be told quite frankly here that the Union-men have actually lived at its expense, and continue to live so; for this public is so magnanimous that, through the frequent purchase of country-corrupting books and journals, it feeds the very poison-distributors <note>original: 'Gift-Spediteurs'</note> of the German nation itself. For if the public did not buy these books, the Union-men would have no work, and would perhaps have to change from 'men of the nation' into soldiers for their princes, and in that way, they would at least become useful people for the state.

The content of this improved plan proves quite tangibly that the **German Union** <note>A radical secret society founded by Karl Friedrich Bahrdt in the late 1780s</note> was not so much a substitute for the **Order of the Illuminati**—which was believed to be extinct—but rather merely its *antechamber*. --- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the membership lists associated with the "Union" or secret societies, following a discussion of the individuals implicated in these organizations.</meta>

However, it became clear through the many subsequent public protests that very few of the men named here were actually <term>Unionists<note>Members of the "German Union," a 18th-century secret society often linked to the Illuminati and radical Enlightenment ideas.</note></term> or even wished to be. It will strike readers as remarkable to find no names from the **Austrian states** here. For well-founded reasons, I have omitted the few found therein—with the exception of Wucherer—because those names were likely false. It must also be noted that these lists were the very first ones, into which everything that even resembled a name was swept up, in order to lie to the gullible about a great army of alleged members. My name appeared nowhere among them.

— Subsequently, other lists did circulate, including some from the Austrian states, but these were kept quite secret and, to my knowledge, have never been printed. For this reason, I will not be the first to have these lists printed either, as they likely also contained several forged names.

Since, however, the list to be shared here was published in the year 1789 by the bookseller Gös <insert>Göschen</insert> <note>Georg Joachim Göschen (1752–1828), a prominent German publisher of the era.</note>

Here, then, my readers have a preliminary, though still incomplete, documentary detail of the constitution, the intentions, and the progress of the **German Union** <note>The Deutsche Union, a 18th-century secret society often linked to radical Enlightenment ideas</note>. It is now time to prove to them to what extent it is connected with the **Order of the Illuminati**, to what extent both work toward the same goals, and whether I was able to say with truth above that the Union is actually the *antechamber* <note>original: "Antichambre"</note> and the great arsenal of the Illuminati Order.

As is well known, the notorious <insert>Dr. Bahrdt</insert> <note>Karl Friedrich Bahrdt (1741–1792), a controversial theologian and writer</note> was one of the first propagators, directors, and recruiters of the Union; but he was not its actual founder, as has been so frequently and erroneously believed. It will be recounted further below from original letters that the two chiefs of the Illuminati, <insert>Knigge and Weishaupt, already fell into discord with one another in the year 1783</insert>, and that Knigge threatened—and also kept his word—to establish his own new society for himself. Furthermore, it is known that these chiefs, along with their entire Order, experienced a great embarrassment when, in the year 1785, the well-known

--- Page 112 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the suppression and subsequent clandestine activities of the Illuminati order following their exposure in Bavaria.</meta>

Inquisition in Bavaria <note>The "Inquisition" here refers to the judicial investigations and bans initiated by Elector Karl Theodor against the Illuminati</note> broke out against them, and in the years 1786 and 1787 their original writings were published in print to all of Germany; for now, several governments finally took the trouble to pay a little attention to this strange institution. Thus, from that point on, they had to seek to lie and affect a pretense everywhere, claiming that their Order was from then on essentially extinct; at the very least, they had to begin to suspend <note>original: "supersediren"</note> new initiations and the senior leadership over German Freemasonry—which had likewise already sent them a formal declaration of outlawry from Berlin—and generally seek to shroud themselves in the cloak of a quiet, creeping incognito.

But that could not possibly be useful for their purposes; rather, they were hindered in them in the most sensitive way, for it was precisely then that the project of the great World Reformation began to ripen in their schools and was promoted toward an outbreak in the fullest earnestness. They had already at that time [gained control] of a large part of the French

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the ideological struggle within German secret societies during the late 18th century, specifically focusing on the tactics used by the Illuminati to suppress their rivals.</meta>

and German <term>Freemasonry</term><note>Freimaurerei: a fraternal organization that became a battleground for competing Enlightenment and mystical ideologies</note> they took possession, but they could not become entirely masters of it; and yet, they still required many and highly enterprising assistants for their plans.

Two things they now had to do: first, to strengthen themselves and their party in every conceivable way; and then, to shatter everything that could possibly be called an opposing party through cunning and force on all sides. The latter they did and achieved by raising, from this time forward in all the cities

--- Page 135 ---
Is it not then clearly evident that factions are the cause when, in such cases, either nothing at all happens, or the exact opposite and the impractical occurs? This happens even where the intention is manifested to exterminate wicked sects—of whom it may be quite well known that they aim for the ruin of religion and governments. Is it not evident that people disdain, or fail to sufficiently appreciate, those very means and tools which alone would be capable of bringing about the fall and the death of these sects? These are—whether one wishes to believe it or not—solely courageous writers of known and famous names, who think honestly and uprightly, who know how to give their pens power and emphasis, and who, through continuous observations and investigations, and through precise and manifold knowledge of secret facts, have informed themselves sufficiently and on the basis of the records <note>original: "aktenmäßig" — meaning documented by official files or evidence</note> regarding the damage of the times and its actual sources: the hidden cliques and sects.

<meta>This page continues the author's reflection on his relationship with the Prussian monarchy and his campaign against the radical Enlightenment, following his previous discussions on political loyalty.</meta>

At one time, it indeed seemed as though the King of Prussia <note>Frederick William II, who reigned from 1786 to 1797</note> remained convinced of this truth. Some writers experienced something akin to encouragement, and I was one of those writers. The King wrote to me on December 28, 1791: He would be grateful to me if I would unmask before the world the secret intrigues of a mob of evil-minded and ill-thinking people in his and other lands, and, to their just shame, expose them in their ugly form to public contempt before the eyes of his other faithful and good subjects. Trusting in such a great royal word, and conscious of my righteous cause, I have courageously and zealously followed all these years that which I was encouraged to do by the King; but I also wished I could boast that this royal encouragement had served me as a protective bulwark against the many abuses and slanders of the **Berlin Clique** <note>A reference to the influential circle of rationalist thinkers and officials in Berlin whom the author opposed</note>.

4) "The stakeholders and reading societies <note>Groups formed to share the cost of books and periodicals, a common practice in the Enlightenment</note> must make their own arrangements with the postal services regarding the delivery of the newspaper. The Directors, however, must ensure—alongside the other local members—that the Intelligence Gazette <note>original: 'Intelligenzblatt'; a term for a newspaper providing useful information and advertisements</note> is recommended and made popular in all households, and specifically that no Union member be found who does not at least share a subscription to the newspaper in a group. For the more readers this Union Intelligence Gazette has, the higher the book sales <note>original: 'Bücherdebit'</note> will rise: the more the audience for every author increases, as does the profit for the treasury."

# XVII.

## E x p e n d i t u r e s.

To this category belong:

1) Payment of the sovereign taxes <note>original: "landesherrlichen Abgaben"</note> to the central authority.
2) Support of talented individuals with money.

The patched-together system of hermetic philosophy <note>A tradition of esoteric knowledge based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note> is unknown to no one who has looked around in the history of philosophy.
